PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended December 31, 1898. 25
194 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEO., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 330. CFEFOO DISTRICT. WEIHAIWEI LIGHTS. THE following Notice to the Fleet, issued by Sir EDWARD SEYMOUR, K.C.B., Commander in Chief of Her Britannic Majesty&#39;s Naval Forces in Chinese Waters, concerning the establish- ment of a light on Flagstaff Point and the re-lighting of Chao-pei-tsui Light, is hereby circulated for general information.- &quot;Centurion, at Weihaiwei, &quot; 4th October 1898. &quot;HYDROGRAPHICAL MEMORANDUM No. 5 &quot;WEIHAIWEI. &quot;I.-LGHr ON FLAGSTAFF PoInT. &quot;A light was exhibited from Flagstaff Point on the 29th September. It is a group occulting red and white light, showing three eclipses every 30 seconds. &quot;The light will show white over the western entrance between the bearings S. 44 W. and S. 52* W., and over the eastern entrance between N. 840 W. and N. 750 W., and red i4 all other directions. &quot;The light is situated on the islet off Flagstaff Point, which is about i mile to E.S.E. of Weihaiwei city. It is elevated 52 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather the white light should be visible io miles, and the red 5 miles. &quot;The light is exhibited from an iron column 121 feet high, and the column and dwelling are white.&quot; &quot;Chao-pei-tsui Light has been re-established and shows as formerly. &quot;(Signed) E. H. SEYMOUR, &#39;Vice-Admiral&quot; SHANGHAI, 24th October 1898.
1898.] NOTIFICATIONS. 195 No. 331. CANTON D ISTRICT. JUNK ROCK BUOY. NoTICE is hereby given that Junk Rock, in Kellett Reach, Blenheim Passage, Canton River, is now marked by a black conical buoy, showing a green light from sunset to sunrise. The buoy is moored on the north-western edge of the rock in 22 feet at low water of spring tides, and must be left on the port hand by vessels passing it from seaward. SHANGHAI, 4th November 1898. APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. OOTOBER-DEOEMBER 1898. 27
210 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT. -DEC., (Received 17th January 1899.) CUTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, ioTn JAvuARY 1899. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 1898, October-December. b. The Custom House (A R ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. .The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Ta 3o and HkMTh 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (&amp;amp; ) Changchow (g g), Fotochow (~ 5 g), and Kowloon (I 3) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; frot China. (eceived 7th January 899.) C O USE IAPPA, IaT JANUAR 1899. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular NO. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter f the year 1898, October-December. b. The Custom House ($ * N) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.f 3o and Hk.t 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- ienced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July j887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (M 1 ) and Chienshan (&#39;p a15) staons, with three sub-stations, known a the Shekkok (5 R), Kuttai (W *), and Kwanchiap ( ) sub-stations. c. Tfie word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. f II.
